\title{Counting derangements}
\author{}
\date{}

\maketitle
\section*{Definitions}
\paragraph{Inst fintype elem perm derangements.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:1}\textup{(D1)} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{inst\,Fintype\,Elem\,Perm\,Derangements} = \operatorname{Subtype\mathord{:}fintype}(x \mapsto \forall x_{1}\, :\, \alpha,\; \neg (\operatorname{coe}(x,\allowbreak{} x_{1}) = x_{1}))\).

\paragraph{Num derangements (\(D\)).} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:2}\textup{(D2)} \(D\left(0\right) = 1\).
\par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:3}\textup{(D3)} \(D\left(1\right) = 0\).
\par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:4}\textup{(D4)} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(D\left(n + 1 + 1\right) = \left(n + 1\right) \cdot \left(D\left(n\right) + D\left(n + 1\right)\right)\).

\subsection*{Prerequisites}
\begin{description}
\item[P1.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}\textbf{To exists sum of at most.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[P2.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:446563696461626c652e6279436f6e74726164696374696f6e}\textbf{By contradiction.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p\) is decidable. Assume \(\neg p \implies \mathrm{False}\). Then \(p\).
\item[P3.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:446973747269622e6c65667444697374726962436c617373}\textbf{Left distrib class.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Distrib}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Left\,Distrib\,Class}\left(R\right)\).
\item[P4.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f65726173655f6f665f6d656d}\textbf{Cardinality erase of membership.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Assume \(a \in s\). Then \(\left|s \setminus \{a\}\right| = \left|s\right| - 1\).
\item[P5.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f66696e}\textbf{Cardinality fin.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\left|\operatorname{Fin}(n)\right| = n\).
\item[P6.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e66696c7465725f6e6527}\textbf{Filter ne'.} Let \(\beta\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\beta\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(\{a\in s \mid a \ne b\} = s \setminus \{b\}\).
\item[P7.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f66696c746572}\textbf{Membership filter.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume the predicate \(p\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in \{x\in s \mid p(x)\} \iff a \in s \land p\left(a\right)\).
\item[P8.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f72616e67655f737563635f696666}\textbf{Membership range successor if and only if.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Then \(a \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<b + 1\} \iff a \le b\).
\item[P9.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976}\textbf{Membership in the full set.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Let \(x\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(x \in \alpha\).
\item[P10.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d756c5f73756d}\textbf{Product sum.} Let \(\iota\) and \(\alpha\) be types. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Non\,Unital\,Non\,Assoc\,Semiring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\iota \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \cdot \sum_{i\in s}\left(f\left(i\right)\right) = \sum_{i\in s}\left(a \cdot f\left(i\right)\right)\).
\item[P11.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Equality of sums with equal summands.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\iota\). Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(f\) and \(g\) be functions of type \(\iota \to M\). Assume \(s_{1} = s_{2}\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \iota,\; x \in s_{2} \implies f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(\sum_{i\in s_{1}}\left(f(i)\right) = \sum_{i\in s_{2}}\left(g(i)\right)\).
\item[P12.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}\textbf{The sum of a constant.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(\sum_{x\in s}\left(b\right) = \left|s\right| \mathbin{\bullet} b\).
\item[P13.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f72616e67655f73756363}\textbf{Sum range successor.} Let \(M\) be a type. Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\mathbb{N} \to M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\sum_{x\in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<n + 1\}}(f(x)) = \sum_{x\in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<n\}}(f(x)) + f(n)\).
\item[P14.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Cardinality congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\beta\). Let \(f\) be a bijection from \(\alpha\) to \(\beta\). Then \(\left|\alpha\right| = \left|\beta\right|\).
\item[P15.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f66696e}\textbf{Cardinality fin.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\left|\operatorname{Fin}(n)\right| = n\).
\item[P16.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f6f6646696e736574}\textbf{Cardinality of finset.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(p\) be a subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(H\) be a proof of \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; x \in s \iff x \in p\). Then \(\left|p\right| = \left|s\right|\).
\item[P17.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f6f7074696f6e}\textbf{Cardinality option.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left|\operatorname{Option}\left(\alpha\right)\right| = \left|\alpha\right| + 1\).
\item[P18.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f7369676d61}\textbf{Cardinality sigma.} Let \(\iota\) be a type. Let \(\alpha\) be a function of type \(\iota \to \mathsf{Type}\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\iota\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\Pi_{i\, :\, \iota}\, \operatorname{Fintype}\left(\alpha\left(i\right)\right)\). Then \(|\operatorname{Sigma}(\alpha)| = \sum_{i\in \iota}(|\alpha(i)|)\).
\item[P19.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f73756d}\textbf{Cardinality sum.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\beta\). Then \(\left|\operatorname{Sum}\left(\alpha, \beta\right)\right| = \left|\alpha\right| + \left|\beta\right|\).
\item[P20.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}\textbf{Inst is strict ordered ring.} \(\mathbb{Z}\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\).
\item[P21.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6e6174436173745f616464}\textbf{Nat cast sum.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Then \(a + b = a + b\).
\item[P22.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6e6174436173745f6d756c}\textbf{Nat cast product.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Then \(a \cdot b = a \cdot b\).
\item[P23.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f616464}\textbf{Of nat sum.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Then \(n + m = n + m\).
\item[P24.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f6f6e65}\textbf{Of nat one.} \(1 = 1\).
\item[P25.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Difference nonnegative of at most.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be integers. Assume \(b \le a\). Then \(0 \le a - b\).
\item[P26.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is left cancel sum.} Let \(G\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\).
\item[P27.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f4973526967687443616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is right cancel sum.} Let \(G\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Right\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\).
\item[P28.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}\textbf{Sum left reflect at most of sum left reflect less than.} Let \(N\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add}\left(N\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(N\right)\). Equip \(N\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LT}\left(N\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LE}\left(N\right)\).
\item[P29.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\textbf{To sum left mono.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Assume \(\text{translation preserves order on }\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[P30.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is cancel sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Assume \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[P31.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{To is ordered cancel sum monoid.} Let \(R\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Semiring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Partial\,Order}\left(R\right)\). Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Then \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }R\).
\item[P32.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e616464496e657175616c6974795f736174}\textbf{Sum inequality sat.} Let \(c\) be an integer. Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Assume \(c + \operatorname{dot}\left(x, y\right) \ge 0\). Then \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(-c),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{none}),\allowbreak{} x,\allowbreak{} y) = \mathrm{true}\).
\item[P33.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e436f6e73747261696e742e6e6f745f736174275f6f665f6973496d706f737369626c65}\textbf{Not sat' of is impossible.} Let \(c\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Constraint}\). Assume \(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}\left(c\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\neg \left(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(c, x, y\right) = \mathrm{true}\right)\).
\item[P34.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Sum congruence.} Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be integers. Assume \(a = b\). Assume \(c = d\). Then \(a + c = b + d\).
\item[P35.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c655f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Of nat at most of at most.} Let \(x\) and \(y\) be natural numbers. Assume \(x \le y\). Then \(x \le y\).
\item[P36.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}\textbf{Difference congruence.} Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be integers. Assume \(a = b\). Assume \(c = d\). Then \(a - c = b - d\).
\item[P37.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c}\textbf{Sum eval.} Let \(l_{1}\) and \(l_{2}\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Linear\,Combo}\). Let \(v\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(l_{1} + l_{2},\allowbreak{} v) = \operatorname{eval}(l_{1},\allowbreak{} v) + \operatorname{eval}(l_{2},\allowbreak{} v)\).
\item[P38.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30}\textbf{Coordinate eval 0.} Let \(\mathit{a0}\) be an integer. Let \(t\) be a list of elements of \(\mathbb{Z}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle \mathit{a0}\rangle \mathbin{\Vert} t)) = \mathit{a0}\).
\item[P39.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c}\textbf{Difference eval.} Let \(l_{1}\) and \(l_{2}\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Linear\,Combo}\). Let \(v\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Then \(\operatorname{eval}(l_{1} - l_{2},\allowbreak{} v) = \operatorname{eval}(l_{1},\allowbreak{} v) - \operatorname{eval}(l_{2},\allowbreak{} v)\).
\item[P40.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e746964795f736174}\textbf{Tidy sat.} Let \(s\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Constraint}\). Let \(x\) and \(v\) be elements of \(\operatorname{Coeffs}\). Assume \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}\left(s, x, v\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(\operatorname{sat^{\prime}}(\operatorname{tidy\,Constraint}(s,\allowbreak{} x),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{tidy\,Coeffs}(s,\allowbreak{} x),\allowbreak{} v) = \mathrm{true}\).
\item[P41.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e6f665f726177}\textbf{Of raw.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be an integer. Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right), n\right)\).
\item[P42.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f69734e6174}\textbf{To is nat.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[P43.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e4973496e742e746f5f7261775f6571}\textbf{To raw equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be an integer. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right)\).
\item[P44.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e6f665f726177}\textbf{Of raw.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right), n\right)\).
\item[P45.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f6973496e74}\textbf{To is int.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[P46.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e49734e61742e746f5f7261775f6571}\textbf{To raw equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right)\).
\item[P47.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f616464}\textbf{Is int sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be integers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x + y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(f\left(a, b\right), c\right)\).
\item[P48.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e6973496e745f6d756c}\textbf{Is int product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be integers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x \cdot y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Int}\left(a \cdot b, c\right)\).
\item[P49.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f616464}\textbf{Is nat sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \alpha \to \alpha\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(a^{'}\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(f = \left(x \mapsto y \mapsto x + y\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, a^{'}\right)\). Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(b, b^{'}\right)\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(f\left(a, b\right), c\right)\).
\item[P50.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6e617443617374}\textbf{Is nat nat cast.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(n, m\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(\operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(n\right), m\right)\).
\item[P51.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}\textbf{Is nat of nat.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}\left(n\right) = a\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[P52.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Sum congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(a + b = c\).
\item[P53.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}\textbf{Sum product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{1} \cdot b = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2} \cdot b = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + c_{2} = d\). Then \(\left(a_{1} + a_{2}\right) \cdot b = d\).
\item[P54.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6f7665726c61705f7066}\textbf{Sum overlap pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(x\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(e\) be a natural number. Assume \(a + b = c\). Then \(x^{e} \cdot a + x^{e} \cdot b = x^{e} \cdot c\).
\item[P55.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}\textbf{Sum pf sum greater than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{2}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a + b_{2} = c\). Then \(a + \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = b_{1} + c\).
\item[P56.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}\textbf{Sum pf sum less than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{2} + b = c\). Then \(a_{1} + a_{2} + b = a_{1} + c\).
\item[P57.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6f7665726c6170}\textbf{Sum pf sum overlap.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b_{1}\), \(b_{2}\), \(c_{1}\), and \(c_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{1} + b_{1} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2} + b_{2} = c_{2}\). Then \(a_{1} + a_{2} + \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = c_{1} + c_{2}\).
\item[P58.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum pf sum zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[P59.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}\textbf{Sum pf zero sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 + b = b\).
\item[P60.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}\textbf{Atom pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a = a^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
\item[P61.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}\textbf{Cast positivity.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(n\right) + 0\).
\item[P62.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}\textbf{Product sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{1}\), \(b_{2}\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{1} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{2} = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + 0 + c_{2} = d\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = d\).
\item[P63.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Product congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(a \cdot b = c\).
\item[P64.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f6f6e65}\textbf{Product one.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) = a\).
\item[P65.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}\textbf{Product pf left.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{3}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(a_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a_{3} \cdot b = c\). Then \(a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot a_{3} \cdot b = a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[P66.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}\textbf{Product pf right.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{3}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a \cdot b_{3} = c\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot b_{3}\right) = b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[P67.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70705f70665f6f7665726c6170}\textbf{Product pp pf overlap.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\), \(b_{2}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(\mathit{ea}\), \(\mathit{eb}\), and \(e\) be natural numbers. Let \(x\) be an element of \(R\). Assume \(\mathit{ea} + \mathit{eb} = e\). Assume \(a_{2} \cdot b_{2} = c\). Then \(x^{\mathit{ea}} \cdot a_{2} \cdot \left(x^{\mathit{eb}} \cdot b_{2}\right) = x^{e} \cdot c\).
\item[P68.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Product zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot 0 = 0\).
\item[P69.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f616464}\textbf{Neg sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b_{1}\), and \(b_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-a_{1} = b_{1}\). Assume \(-a_{2} = b_{2}\). Then \(-\left(a_{1} + a_{2}\right) = b_{1} + b_{2}\).
\item[P70.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f636f6e6772}\textbf{Neg congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(-a^{'} = b\). Then \(-a = b\).
\item[P71.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6d756c}\textbf{Neg product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a_{1}\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(a_{2}\) be a natural number. Let \(a_{3}\) and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-a_{3} = b\). Then \(-\left(a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot a_{3}\right) = a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot b\).
\item[P72.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{Neg one product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}\left(1\right)\right) \cdot a = b\). Then \(-a = b\).
\item[P73.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6e65675f7a65726f}\textbf{Neg zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Then \(-0 = 0\).
\item[P74.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}\textbf{Of equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a = c\). Assume \(b = c\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[P75.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{One product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) \cdot a = a\).
\item[P76.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}\textbf{Power sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(c_{1}\), and \(c_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b_{1}\) and \(b_{2}\) be natural numbers. Let \(d\) be an element of \(R\). Assume \(a^{b_{1}} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a^{b_{2}} = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} \cdot c_{2} = d\). Then \(a^{b_{1} + b_{2}} = d\).
\item[P77.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}\textbf{Power congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b\) and \(b^{'}\) be natural numbers. Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \({a^{'}}^{b^{'}} = c\). Then \(a^{b} = c\).
\item[P78.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f70726f645f61746f6d}\textbf{Power product atom.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(b\) be a natural number. Then \(a^{b} = \left(a + 0\right)^{b} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\).
\item[P79.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}\textbf{Power zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a^{0} = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
\item[P80.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}\textbf{Single power.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b\) be a natural number. Assume \(a^{b} = c\). Then \(\left(a + 0\right)^{b} = c + 0\).
\item[P81.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f636f6e6772}\textbf{Difference congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} - b^{'} = c\). Then \(a - b = c\).
\item[P82.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f7066}\textbf{Difference pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Ring}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(-b = c\). Assume \(a + c = d\). Then \(a - b = d\).
\item[P83.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}\textbf{Zero product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 \cdot b = 0\).
\item[P84.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e617363466163746f7269616c5f73756363}\textbf{Asc factorial successor.} Let \(n\) and \(k\) be natural numbers. Then \(\operatorname{asc\,Factorial}(n,\allowbreak{} k + 1) = (n + k) \cdot \operatorname{asc\,Factorial}(n,\allowbreak{} k)\).
\item[P85.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e617363466163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Asc factorial zero.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{asc\,Factorial}\left(n, 0\right) = 1\).
\item[P86.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e636173745f6f6e65}\textbf{Cast one.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{cast}_{R}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\item[P87.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}\textbf{Inst canonically ordered sum.} \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[P88.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{Inst is ordered sum monoid.} \(\text{translation preserves order on }\mathbb{N}\).
\item[P89.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{Inst is ordered cancel sum monoid.} \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\mathbb{N}\).
\item[P90.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}\textbf{Inst ordered difference.} \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[P91.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6e6f745f6c74}\textbf{At most of not less than.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Assume \(\neg \left(a < b\right)\). Then \(b \le a\).
\item[P92.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e737563635f737562}\textbf{Successor difference.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \le m\). Then \(m + 1 - n = m - n + 1\).
\item[P93.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Inst subsingleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Sort}\). Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be a proof of \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; \beta\left(a\right)\text{ has at most one element}\). Then \(\left(\Pi_{a\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(a\right)\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\item[P94.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5365742e6d656d5f636f6d706c5f73696e676c65746f6e5f696666}\textbf{Membership compl singleton if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) and \(x\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c} \iff x \ne a\).
\item[P95.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}\textbf{Elim.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Assume \(\alpha\text{ has at most one element}\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[P96.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f6c6566745f696e6a}\textbf{Sum left injectivity.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Right\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(b + a = c + a \iff b = c\).
\item[P97.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f72696768745f636f6d6d}\textbf{Sum right commutation.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Semigroup}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a + b + c = a + c + b\).
\item[P98.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Sum tsub cancel of at most.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Semigroup}\left(\alpha\right)\). Equip \(\alpha\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{5}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \le b\). Then \(a + \left(b - a\right) = b\).
\item[P99.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}\textbf{Sum tsub cancel right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a partial order. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Semigroup}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a + b - b = a\).
\item[P100.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}\textbf{Contravariant less than of covariant at most.} Let \(N\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{mu}\) be a function of type \(N \to N \to N\). Equip \(N\) with a linear order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Covariant\,Class}(N,\allowbreak{} N,\allowbreak{} \mathit{mu},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} \le \mathit{x2})\). Then \(\operatorname{Contravariant\,Class}(N,\allowbreak{} N,\allowbreak{} \mathit{mu},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} < \mathit{x2})\).
\item[P101.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f74727565}\textbf{Equals true.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p\). Then \(p = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[P102.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Forall congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) and \(q\) be functions of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) = q\left(a\right)\). Then \((\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a)) = (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; q(a))\).
\item[P103.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:66756e657874}\textbf{Function extensionality.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Sort}\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Let \(g\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(f = g\).
\item[P104.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6966665f73656c66}\textbf{If and only if self.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(p \iff p\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[P105.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696d706c6965735f74727565}\textbf{Implies true.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Then \(\left(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; \mathrm{True}\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[P106.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}\textbf{Inst subsingleton decidable.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(p\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\item[P107.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6c655f6f665f6c655f6f665f6571}\textbf{At most of at most of equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Assume \(a \le b\). Assume \(b = c\). Then \(a \le c\).
\item[P108.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f616464}\textbf{Product sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul}\left(R\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Left\,Distrib\,Class}\left(R\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b + c\right) = a \cdot b + a \cdot c\).
\item[P109.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f6c6566745f636f6d6d}\textbf{Multiplication left commutativity.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Semigroup}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b \cdot c\right) = b \cdot \left(a \cdot c\right)\).
\item[P110.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}\textbf{Product one.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,One\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a \cdot 1 = a\).
\item[P111.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e65675f6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{Neg one product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,One\,Class}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Has\,Distrib\,Neg}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(-1 \cdot a = -a\).
\item[P112.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e736d756c5f65715f6d756c}\textbf{Repeated addition equals product.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Non\,Assoc\,Semiring}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(n \mathbin{\bullet} a = \operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}\left(n\right) \cdot a\).
\item[P113.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6f665f6465636964655f65715f74727565}\textbf{Of decide equals true.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p\) is decidable. Assume \(\operatorname{decide}\left(p\right) = \mathrm{true}\). Then \(p\).
\item[P114.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:706f775f73756363}\textbf{Power successor.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(a^{n + 1} = a^{n} \cdot a\).
\item[P115.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:706f775f7375636327}\textbf{Power succ'.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(a^{n + 1} = a \cdot a^{n}\).
\item[P116.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7375625f65715f6164645f6e6567}\textbf{Difference equals sum neg.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub\,Neg\,Monoid}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a - b = a + -b\).
\item[P117.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747275655f616e64}\textbf{True and.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(\mathrm{True} \land p\right) = p\).
\item[P118.] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747375625f73656c66}\textbf{Tsub self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a - a = 0\).
\end{description}

\section{Results}
\begin{lemma}[{Cardinality derangements invariant}]\label{theorem:1}
Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\beta\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Assume \(\left|\alpha\right| = \left|\beta\right|\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle |\operatorname{derangements}(\alpha)| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\beta)|.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The supplied bijection \(\operatorname{derangements\,Congr}\left(\operatorname{equiv\,Of\,Card\,Eq}\right)\) using \(\left|\alpha\right| = \left|\beta\right|\) maps \(\operatorname{derangements}\left(\alpha\right)\) onto \(\operatorname{derangements}\left(\beta\right)\). By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e6772}, their cardinalities satisfy \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\alpha)| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\beta)|\).

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Cardinality derangements fin sum two}]\label{theorem:2}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 2))| = (n + 1) \cdot |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n))| + (n + 1) \cdot |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(a\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f66696c746572} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\) and \(s = \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\), we obtain \(x \in \{x_{1}\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \mid (y \mapsto y \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(x_{1})\} \iff x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((x \in \{x_{1}\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \mid (y \mapsto y \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(x_{1})\}) = (x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\), we obtain \(x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f74727565}, we obtain \(\left(x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}) = (\mathrm{True} \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:747275655f616e64} with \(p = x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\), we obtain \(\left(\mathrm{True} \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right) = \left(x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \land x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}) = (x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((x \in \{x_{1}\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \mid (y \mapsto y \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(x_{1})\}) = (x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((x \in \{x_{1}\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \mid (y \mapsto y \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(x_{1})\} \iff x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}) = (x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c} \iff x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6966665f73656c66} with \(p = x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\), we obtain \(\left(x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c} \iff x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((x \in \{x_{1}\in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1) \mid (y \mapsto y \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(x_{1})\} \iff x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}) = \mathrm{True}\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(\mathit{inst}\) in \(\operatorname{Decidable\,Pred}\left(x \mapsto x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(s\) in \(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))\).
Fix \(s_{1}\) in \(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))\).
Assume \(s = s_{1}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\{x\in s \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\} = \{x\in s \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\}\).
Use the hypothesis \(s = s_{1}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\{x\in s \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\} = \{x\in s_{1} \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\}\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65} with \(p = \left(x \mapsto x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\left(a_{1}\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Decidable}((x \mapsto x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{1}\, :\, \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)}\, (a_{2} \mapsto \operatorname{Decidable}((x \mapsto x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c})(a_{2})))(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Decidable\,Pred}\left(x \mapsto x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\) and \(\mathit{inst}\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = \mathit{inst}_{1}\), we obtain \(\mathit{inst} = \mathit{inst}_{1}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \((\mathit{inst}_{2} \mapsto \forall s\, :\, \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)),\; \forall s_{1}\, :\, \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)),\; s = s_{1} \implies \{x\in s \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\} = \{x\in s_{1} \mid x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\})(\mathit{inst}_{1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:5365742e6d656d5f636f6d706c5f73696e676c65746f6e5f696666} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\), we obtain \(x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c} \iff x \ne a\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \(\left(x \in \{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right) = \left(x \ne a\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f6f6646696e736574}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696d706c6965735f74727565}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:66756e657874}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e66696c7465725f6e6527}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f65726173655f6f665f6d656d}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f66696e}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f66696e}, the two identities established above and the auxiliary result established above gives \(\left|\{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right| = \left|\operatorname{Fin}(n)\right|\).
Rewriting with Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f66696e} and Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f6f7074696f6e} gives \(|\operatorname{Fin}(n + 2)| = |\operatorname{Option}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\).
The supplied bijection \(\operatorname{derangements\,Recursion\,Equiv}\) specialized to \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\) maps \(\operatorname{derangements}\left(\operatorname{Option}\left(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\right)\right)\) onto the pairs with first component \(a\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\) and second component in the disjoint union of \(\operatorname{derangements}\left(\{a\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\) and \(\operatorname{derangements}\left(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\right)\). By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e6772}, the two finite types have equal cardinality.
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
Assume \(x \in \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f73756d} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{derangements}\left(\{x\}^{\mathrm c}\right)\) and \(\beta = \operatorname{derangements}\left(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)\right)\), we obtain \(|\operatorname{Sum}(\operatorname{derangements}(\{x\}^{\mathrm c}),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)))| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\{x\}^{\mathrm c})| + |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\).
Applying the hypothesis \(\forall a\, :\, \operatorname{Fin}(n + 1),\; |\{a\}^{\mathrm c}| = |\operatorname{Fin}(n)|\), we obtain \(\left|\{x\}^{\mathrm c}\right| = \left|\operatorname{Fin}(n)\right|\).
By Lemma~\ref{theorem:1} with \(\alpha = \{x\}^{\mathrm c}\) and \(\beta = \operatorname{Fin}(n)\), we obtain \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\{x\}^{\mathrm c})| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n))|\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\{x\}^{\mathrm c})| + |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n))| + |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(|\operatorname{Sum}(\operatorname{derangements}(\{x\}^{\mathrm c}),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1)))| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n))| + |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with Lemma~\ref{theorem:1} (using \(|\operatorname{Fin}(n + 2)| = |\operatorname{Option}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\)), Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f7369676d61}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e7374}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f66696e}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e736d756c5f65715f6d756c}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f616464}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:446973747269622e6c65667444697374726962436c617373} and the two identities established above gives \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 2))| = (n + 1) \cdot |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n))| + (n + 1) \cdot |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(n + 1))|\).

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{Num derangements zero}]\label{theorem:3}
\(D(0) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Evaluating the two sides gives \(D(0) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{Num derangements one}]\label{theorem:4}
\(D(1) = 0\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Evaluating the two sides gives \(D(1) = 0\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Num derangements sum two}]\label{theorem:5}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(D(n + 2) = (n + 1) \cdot (D(n) + D(n + 1))\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Evaluating the two sides gives
\[
D(n + 2) = (n + 1) \cdot (D(n) + D(n + 1)).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Num derangements successor}]\label{theorem:6}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(D\left(n + 1\right) = \left(n + 1\right) \cdot D\left(n\right) - \left(-1\right)^{n}\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Proceed by induction on \(n\).
\emph{Base case.}
\begin{proofstep}
At zero, \(D\left(0 + 1\right) = \left(0 + 1\right) \cdot D\left(0\right) - \left(-1\right)^{0}\) holds by definition.
\end{proofstep}
\emph{Successor step.}
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(n_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume as induction hypothesis that \(D\left(n_{1} + 1\right) = \left(n_{1} + 1\right) \cdot D\left(n_{1}\right) - \left(-1\right)^{n_{1}}\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = n_{1}\), we obtain \(n_{1} = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(1, 1\right)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6e617443617374} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) and \(m = 1\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(\operatorname{cast}_{\mathbb{Z}}\left(1\right), 1\right)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\), we obtain \(n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + 0 = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(b_{1} = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(n_{1} + 1 = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = D\left(n_{1}\right)\), we obtain \(D(n_{1}) = D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = n_{1}\), we obtain \(n_{1} = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(1, 1\right)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\), we obtain \(n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + 0 = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(b_{1} = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + (\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \(n_{1} + 1 = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = D\left(n_{1}\right)\), we obtain \(D(n_{1}) = D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(a = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\) and \(b_{1} = D\left(n_{1}\right)\) and \(b_{2} = \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)) = D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)\).

((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0)^{n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0) = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(3)) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot ((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0)^{n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1))) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2)} \cdot (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)) + (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2) + ((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0)^{n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0))))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7375625f636f6e6772} with \(R = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \((n_{1} + 1 + 1) \cdot ((n_{1} + 1) \cdot D(n_{1}) - (-1)^{n_{1}}) - (-1)^{n_{1}} \cdot -1 = n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(3)) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot ((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0)^{n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1))) + (n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2)} \cdot (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)) + (D(n_{1})^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(2) + ((\operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0)^{n_{1}^{\operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Nat\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{Int\mathord{:}raw\,Cast}(\operatorname{neg\,Of\,Nat}(1)) + 0))))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{Z}\), we obtain \((n_{1} + 1) \cdot (D(n_{1}) + ((n_{1} + 1) \cdot D(n_{1}) - (-1)^{n_{1}})) = (n_{1} + 1 + 1) \cdot ((n_{1} + 1) \cdot D(n_{1}) - (-1)^{n_{1}}) - (-1)^{n_{1}} \cdot -1\).
Rewriting with Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6e6174436173745f6d756c}, Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6e6174436173745f616464}, \(D\left(n_{1} + 1\right) = \left(n_{1} + 1\right) \cdot D\left(n_{1}\right) - \left(-1\right)^{n_{1}}\) and Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:706f775f73756363} in the preceding result gives \(D(n_{1} + 1 + 1) = (n_{1} + 1 + 1) \cdot D(n_{1} + 1) - (-1)^{n_{1} + 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
By induction, \(D\left(n + 1\right) = \left(n + 1\right) \cdot D\left(n\right) - \left(-1\right)^{n}\).

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Cardinality derangements fin equals num derangements}]\label{theorem:7}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \left|\operatorname{derangements}\left(\operatorname{Fin}(n)\right)\right| = D\left(n\right).\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(n_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m < n_{1} \implies |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(m))| = D(m)\).
In the zero case,
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m < 0 \implies |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(m))| = D(m)\).
By definition, we obtain \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(0))| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(0))|\).
\end{proofstep}
In the successor case,
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(n_{2}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m < n_{2} + 1 \implies |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(m))| = D(m)\).
In the zero case,
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m < 0 + 1 \implies |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(m))| = D(m)\).
By definition, we obtain \(|\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(0 + 1))| = |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(0 + 1))|\).
\end{proofstep}
In the successor case,
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(n_{3}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(\forall m\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; m < n_{3} + 1 + 1 \implies |\operatorname{derangements}(\operatorname{Fin}(m))| = D(m)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(n_{3} + 1 < n_{3} + 1 + 1\right)\).
By definition, we obtain \(\mathrm{true} = \mathrm{true}\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \(\operatorname{decide}(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{some}(0))) = \mathrm{true}) = \mathrm{true}\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f665f6465636964655f65715f74727565}, we obtain \(\operatorname{is\,Impossible}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Constraint\mathord{:}mk}(\operatorname{some}(1),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{some}(0))) = \mathrm{true}\).
Use the hypothesis \(\neg \left(n_{3} + 1 < n_{3} + 1 + 1\right)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6e6f745f6c74}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 \le n_{3} + 1\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6f664e61745f6c655f6f665f6c65}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 \le n_{3} + 1\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e7375625f6e6f6e6e65675f6f665f6c65}, we obtain \(0 \le n_{3} + 1 - \left(n_{3} + 1 + 1\right)\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f616464} with \(n_{3}\) in place of its parameter \(n\) and \(m = 1\), we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = n_{3} + 1\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30} in reverse gives \(n_{3} = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle n_{3}\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f616464} with \(n_{3} + 1\) in place of its parameter \(n\) and \(m = 1\), we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 = n_{3} + 1 + 1\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:496e742e6f664e61745f616464} with \(n_{3}\) in place of its parameter \(n\) and \(m = 1\), we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = n_{3} + 1\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e636f6f7264696e6174655f6576616c5f30} in reverse gives \(n_{3} = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{coordinate}(0),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle n_{3}\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \(1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e6164645f636f6e6772}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e6164645f6576616c} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle n_{3}\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) + \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 + 1 = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
By Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e496e742e7375625f636f6e6772}, we obtain \(n_{3} + 1 - (n_{3} + 1 + 1) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(0,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) - \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) + \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle\,\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
Using Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4c65616e2e4f6d6567612e4c696e656172436f6d626f2e7375625f6576616c} with \(l_{1} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(l_{2} = \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(2,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle)\) and \(v = \operatorname{of\,List}\left(\langle n_{3}\rangle\right)\) in reverse gives \(\operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) - \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(2,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle)) = \operatorname{eval}(\operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(1,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle) - \operatorname{Lean\mathord{:}Omega\mathord{:}Linear\,Combo\mathord{:}mk}(2,\allowbreak{} \langle 1\rangle),\allowbreak{} \operatorname{of\,List}(\langle n_{3}\rangle))\).
